The city's oldest park is the [Hofgarten](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hofgarten_(Munich) \"Hofgarten (Munich)\"), near the Residenz, dating back to the 16th century. The site of the largest beer garden in town, the former royal Hirschgarten, was founded in 1780.[[123]](#cite_note-125)\n\n## Culture\n\n[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Munich&action=edit&section=28 \"Edit section: Culture\")]\n\n### Language\n\n[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Munich&action=edit&section=29 \"Edit section: Language\")]\n\nMain article: [Bavarian language](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bavarian_language \"Bavarian language\")\n\nGerman is spoken and understood in and around Munich. While the German language has many dialects, so-called \"[Standard German](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Standard_German \"Standard German\")\" or \"High German\" is learned in schools and spoken among Germans, Austrians and in some parts of Switzerland. A speaker of a [Low German](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Low_German \"Low German\") dialect in Hamburg may find it difficult to understand the dialect of a Bavarian mountaineer.[[124]](#cite_note-126) The [Bavarian dialects](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bavarian_language \"Bavarian language\") are recognized as regional languages and continue to be spoken alongside Standard German.[[125]](#cite_note-127)\n\n### Museums\n\n[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Munich&action=edit&section=30 \"Edit section: Museums\")]\n\n[![](//thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/14/Deutsches_Museum_Portrait_4.jpg/250px-Deutsches_Museum_Portrait_4.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Deutsches_Museum_Portrait_4.jpg)\n\nThe [Deutsches Museum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Deutsches_Museum \"Deutsches Museum\") is the world's largest museum of [science](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Science_museum \"Science museum\") and [technology](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Technology_museum \"Technology museum\").\n\n#### Technical Museums and Natural History Museums\n\n[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Munich&action=edit&section=31 \"Edit section: Technical Museums and Natural History Museums\")]\n\nIn 1903 [Oskar von Miller](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oskar_von_Miller \"Oskar von Miller\") assembled a group of engineers and industrialists, who chartered the [Deutsches Museum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Deutsches_Museum \"Deutsches Museum\"). The Museum was built with the financial support of the German business and imperial nobility community, as well as the blessing of [Wilhelm II](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wilhelm_II \"Wilhelm II\"), German Emperor.[[126]](#cite_note-128) The Deutsches Museum had its grand opening in 1925; it is the world's largest museum of [science](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Science_museum \"Science museum\") and [technology](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Technology_museum \"Technology museum\").[[127]](#cite_note-129) The Deutsches Museum now operates three locations, with exhibition space at [Flugwerft Schleissheim](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Deutsches_Museum_Flugwerft_Schleissheim \"Deutsches Museum Flugwerft Schleissheim\") and a transportation museum near Theresienwiese. The original site in central Munich continues to expand its exhibits.[[128]](#cite_note-130) Further transportation and technical museums include the [MVG Museum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MVG_Museum \"MVG Museum\") and the [BMW Museum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BMW_Museum \"BMW Museum\").\n\nThere are several natural history museums in Munich. These include the [Museum of Man and Nature](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Museum_of_Man_and_Nature \"Museum of Man and Nature\"), the [Palaeontological Museum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palaeontological_Museum,_Munich \"Palaeontological Museum, Munich\"), the [Museum Reich der Kristalle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Museum_Reich_der_Kristalle \"Museum Reich der Kristalle\"), the [Bavarian State Archaeological Collection](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bavarian_State_Archaeological_Collection \"Bavarian State Archaeological Collection\"), the [Bavarian State Collection of Zoology](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bavarian_State_Collection_of_Zoology \"Bavarian State Collection of Zoology\"), the [Geologisches Museum München](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Geologisches_Museum_München \"Geologisches Museum München\"), the [Botanische Staatsammlung München](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Botanische_Staatssammlung_München \"Botanische Staatssammlung München\"), and the [German Hunting and Fishing Museum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/German_Hunting_and_Fishing_Museum \"German Hunting and Fishing Museum\").\n\n#### Art Museums and Galleries\n\n[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Munich&action=edit&section=32 \"Edit section: Art Museums and Galleries\")]\n\n[![](//thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/45/Bayerisches_Nationalmuseum_-_Muenchen_-_2013.jpg/250px-Bayerisches_Nationalmuseum_-_Muenchen_-_2013.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Bayerisches_Nationalmuseum_-_Muenchen_-_2013.jpg)\n\n[Bavarian National Museum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bavarian_National_Museum \"Bavarian National Museum\")\n\nCity guides published in the early 1860s directed tourists to Munich's architecture and art collections, which at the time were unique in Germany and are a legacy mainly of [Ludwig I of Bavaria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ludwig_I_of_Bavaria \"Ludwig I of Bavaria\"), with contributions from [Maximilian II of Bavaria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maximilian_II_of_Bavaria \"Maximilian II of Bavaria\").[[129]](#cite_note-131) After the first German art exhibition in the [Glaspalast](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Glaspalast_(Munich) \"Glaspalast (Munich)\") for an international audience in 1869, Munich emerged as a focal point for the arts. Men of distinction from around the world visited the [Academy of Fine Arts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Academy_of_Fine_Arts,_Munich \"Academy of Fine Arts, Munich\") under the directorship of [Karl von Piloty](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Karl_von_Piloty \"Karl von Piloty\") and later [Wilhelm von Kaulbach](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wilhelm_von_Kaulbach \"Wilhelm von Kaulbach\").[[130]](#cite_note-132)\n\nThe city has several important art museums and galleries, many of which can be found in the [Kunstareal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kunstareal \"Kunstareal\"). This neighborhood includes several of the galleries of the [Bavarian State Painting Collections](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bavarian_State_Painting_Collections \"Bavarian State Painting Collections\"), most notably the three \"Pinakotheken\" ([Alte Pinakothek](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alte_Pinakothek \"Alte Pinakothek\"), [Neue Pinakothek](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Neue_Pinakothek \"Neue Pinakothek\"), and [Pinakothek der Moderne](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pinakothek_der_Moderne \"Pinakothek der Moderne\")). The [Alte Pinakothek](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alte_Pinakothek \"Alte Pinakothek\") contains works of European masters between the 14th and 18th centuries. Major displays include [Albrecht Dürer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Albrecht_Dürer \"Albrecht Dürer\")'s [*Self-Portrait* (1500)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Self-Portrait_(Dürer,_Munich) \"Self-Portrait (Dürer, Munich)\"), his *[Four Apostles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Four_Apostles \"The Four Apostles\")*, [Raphael](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Raphael \"Raphael\")'s paintings *[The Canigiani Holy Family](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Canigiani_Holy_Family_(Raphael) \"Canigiani Holy Family (Raphael)\")* and *[Madonna Tempi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tempi_Madonna_(Raphael) \"Tempi Madonna (Raphael)\")* as well as [Peter Paul Rubens](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Peter_Paul_Rubens \"Peter Paul Rubens\") large *Judgment Day*. The [Neue Pinakothek](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Neue_Pinakothek \"Neue Pinakothek\"), like other German museums, was wholly reconstructed from 1974 until 1981.[[131]](#cite_note-133) The [Pinakothek der Moderne](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pinakothek_der_Moderne \"Pinakothek der Moderne\") exhibits modern art, architecture, design, and works on paper. The principal focus of its permanent collection is Classical Modernism, but the displays are enhanced continuously with gifts from private collections.[[132]](#cite_note-134) Also included in the Bavarian State Painting Collections are the [Museum Brandhorst](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Museum_Brandhorst \"Museum Brandhorst\"), a modern and contemporary art museum in the Kunstareal, and the [Schackgalerie](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Schackgalerie \"Schackgalerie\"), an important gallery of German 19th-century paintings.[[133]](#cite_note-135)\n\n[![](//thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/c9/Glyptothek_in_M%C3%BCnchen_in_2013.jpg/250px-Glyptothek_in_M%C3%BCnchen_in_2013.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Glyptothek_in_München_in_2013.jpg)\n\nThe [Glyptothek](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Glyptothek \"Glyptothek\") houses King [Ludwig I](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ludwig_I \"Ludwig I\")'s collection of Greek and Roman sculptures.\n\nSeveral further museums are located in the Kunstareal neighborhood. The [Lenbachhaus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lenbachhaus \"Lenbachhaus\") displays works of the movement [Der Blaue Reiter](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Der_Blaue_Reiter \"Der Blaue Reiter\") (The Blue Rider), a Munich-based modernist art.[[134]](#cite_note-136) Another museum is the [Staatliche Sammlung für Ägyptische Kunst](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Staatliche_Sammlung_für_Ägyptische_Kunst \"Staatliche Sammlung für Ägyptische Kunst\") (the State Collection of Egyptian Art).[[135]](#cite_note-137)[[136]](#cite_note-138)[[137]](#cite_note-139) An extensive collection of Greek and Roman art is held in the [Glyptothek](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Glyptothek \"Glyptothek\")[[138]](#cite_note-140) and the [Staatliche Antikensammlungen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Staatliche_Antikensammlungen \"Staatliche Antikensammlungen\") (the State Antiquities Collections). Works on display include the [Medusa Rondanini](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Medusa_Rondanini \"Medusa Rondanini\"), the [Barberini Faun](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Barberini_Faun \"Barberini Faun\") and figures from the [Temple of Aphaea](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Temple_of_Aphaea \"Temple of Aphaea\") on [Aegina](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aegina \"Aegina\") for the Glyptothek.[[139]](#cite_note-141) The [Staatliche Graphische Sammlung](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Staatliche_Graphische_Sammlung_München \"Staatliche Graphische Sammlung München\"), the [Zentralinstitut für Kunstgeschichte](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zentralinstitut_für_Kunstgeschichte \"Zentralinstitut für Kunstgeschichte\"), and the [Museum für Abgüsse Klassischer Bildwerke](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Museum_für_Abgüsse_Klassischer_Bildwerke \"Museum für Abgüsse Klassischer Bildwerke\") are also housed in the building of the former [Munich Central Collecting Point](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Munich_Central_Collecting_Point \"Munich Central Collecting Point\") at [Königsplatz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Königsplatz,_Munich \"Königsplatz, Munich\").\n\nElsewhere in the city are many other notable public art collections, such as the [Haus der Kunst](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Haus_der_Kunst \"Haus der Kunst\"), the [Bavarian National Museum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bavarian_National_Museum \"Bavarian National Museum\"), and the [Villa Stuck](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Villa_Stuck \"Villa Stuck\"). There are also several private collections and art exhibition spaces, such as the [Goetz Collection](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Goetz_Collection \"Goetz Collection\"), the [Museum of Urban and Contemporary Art](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Museum_of_Urban_and_Contemporary_Art \"Museum of Urban and Contemporary Art\"), the [Kunsthalle der Hypo-Kulturstiftung](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kunsthalle_der_Hypo-Kulturstiftung?action=edit&redlink=1 \"Kunsthalle der Hypo-Kulturstiftung (page does not exist)\"), and [Lothringer13](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lothringer13?action=edit&redlink=1 \"Lothringer13 (page does not exist)\").\n\n#### Cultural Museums and Memorial Sites\n\n[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Munich&action=edit&section=33 \"Edit section: Cultural Museums and Memorial Sites\")]\n\nThe [Munich City Museum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Munich_Stadtmuseum \"Munich Stadtmuseum\") is located the old gothic arsenal building in the inner city and contains a variety of cultural collections and museums, such as the [Munich Film Archive](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Munich_Film_Archive \"Munich Film Archive\"). The main museum building is closed for renovations and is scheduled to reopen in summer 2031.[[140]](#cite_note-142) Across the Sankt-Jakobs-Platz from the city museum is the [Jewish Museum Munich](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jewish_Museum_Munich \"Jewish Museum Munich\"), located next to the [Ohel Jakob Synagogue](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ohel_Jakob_synagogue_(Munich) \"Ohel Jakob synagogue (Munich)\").\n\nThe [Museum Five Continents](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Museum_Five_Continents \"Museum Five Continents\") is the second largest collection in Germany of artefacts and objects from outside Europe, while the [Bavarian National Museum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bavarian_National_Museum \"Bavarian National Museum\") and the adjoining [Bavarian State Archaeological Collection](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bavarian_State_Archaeological_Collection \"Bavarian State Archaeological Collection\") display regional art and cultural history. Throughout the city are several smaller museums focused on various historical and cultural aspects, such as the [Sudeten German Museum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sudeten_German_Museum \"Sudeten German Museum\"), the [Beer and Oktoberfest Museum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beer_and_Oktoberfest_Museum \"Beer and Oktoberfest Museum\"), the [Deutsches Brauereimuseum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Deutsches_Brauereimuseum \"Deutsches Brauereimuseum\"), the [Deutsches Theatermuseum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Deutsches_Theatermuseum \"Deutsches Theatermuseum\"), the [Valentin-Karlstadt-Musäum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Valentin-Karlstadt-Musäum?action=edit&redlink=1 \"Valentin-Karlstadt-Musäum (page does not exist)\"), and the [Alpine Museum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alpine_Museum \"Alpine Museum\").\n\nSeveral former residences of the [Wittelsbach](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/House_of_Wittelsbach \"House of Wittelsbach\") monarchs of Bavaria located in the city can be visited. The [Residenz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Munich_Residenz \"Munich Residenz\"), the former royal palace, houses a museum, treasury, the [Cuvilliés Theatre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cuvilliés_Theatre \"Cuvilliés Theatre\"), and the Bavarian state coin collection. The royal family's former summer residence, the Nymphenburg Palace, also includes space for the [Marstallmuseum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marstallmuseum \"Marstallmuseum\") and the [Nymphenburg Porcelain Manufactory](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nymphenburg_Porcelain_Manufactory \"Nymphenburg Porcelain Manufactory\"). Just outside the city limits is an additional summer place complex, [Schleissheim Palace](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Schleissheim_Palace \"Schleissheim Palace\").\n\nSeveral museums and memorials attest to the city's complicated history under National Socialism. In May 2015, the [Munich Documentation Centre for the History of National Socialism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Munich_Documentation_Centre_for_the_History_of_National_Socialism \"Munich Documentation Centre for the History of National Socialism\") opened to the public, and the memorial museum of the former [Dachau concentration camp](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dachau_concentration_camp \"Dachau concentration camp\") is just outside the city. The [Erinnerungsort Olympia-Attentat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Erinnerungsort_Olympia-Attentat \"Erinnerungsort Olympia-Attentat\") further commemorates the victims of the [Munich massacre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Munich_massacre \"Munich massacre\") during the [1972 Summer Olympics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1972_Summer_Olympics \"1972 Summer Olympics\").\n\n### Music\n\n[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Munich&action=edit&section=34 \"Edit section: Music\")]\n\nMunich is a major international musical centre and has played host to many prominent composers including [Orlande de Lassus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Orlande_de_Lassus \"Orlande de Lassus\"), [Orlando di Lasso](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Orlando_di_Lasso \"Orlando di Lasso\"), [Wolfgang Amadeus Mozart](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wolfgang_Amadeus_Mozart \"Wolfgang Amadeus Mozart\"), [Carl Maria von Weber](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Carl_Maria_von_Weber \"Carl Maria von Weber\"), [Richard Wagner](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Richard_Wagner \"Richard Wagner\"), [Gustav Mahler](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gustav_Mahler \"Gustav Mahler\"), [Richard Strauss](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Richard_Strauss \"Richard Strauss\"), [Max Reger](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Max_Reger \"Max Reger\") and [Carl Orff](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Carl_Orff \"Carl Orff\").[[141]](#cite_note-143)[[142]](#cite_note-144) Some notable classical music compositions created in and around Munich include Richard Strauss's tone poem *[Also sprach Zarathustra](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Also_sprach_Zarathustra_(Strauss) \"Also sprach Zarathustra (Strauss)\")*[[143]](#cite_note-145) or Carl Orff's *[Carmina Burana](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Carmina_Burana_(Orff) \"Carmina Burana (Orff)\")*.[[144]](#cite_note-146)[[145]](#cite_note-147)\n\n#### Opera and Orchestral Music\n\n[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Munich&action=edit&section=35 \"Edit section: Opera and Orchestral Music\")]\n\n[![](//thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/7a/Nationaltheater_Munich.jpg/250px-Nationaltheater_Munich.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Nationaltheater_Munich.jpg)\n\nThe [National Theatre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Theatre_Munich \"National Theatre Munich\") is home to the [Bavarian State Opera](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bavarian_State_Opera \"Bavarian State Opera\") and the [Bavarian State Orchestra](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bavarian_State_Orchestra \"Bavarian State Orchestra\").\n\nWhile [Richard Wagner](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Richard_Wagner \"Richard Wagner\") was also a supporter of [William I, German Emperor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/William_I,_German_Emperor \"William I, German Emperor\"), he was generously supported by [Ludwig II of Bavaria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ludwig_II_of_Bavaria \"Ludwig II of Bavaria\").[[146]](#cite_note-148) Ludwig II sponsored the successful Munich premiere of Wagner's *[Die Meistersinger von Nürnberg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Die_Meistersinger_von_Nürnberg \"Die Meistersinger von Nürnberg\")* (*The Mastersingers of Nuremberg*) at the Hoftheater, now the [National Theatre Munich](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Theatre_Munich \"National Theatre Munich\").[[147]](#cite_note-149)\n\n[![](//thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/06/Das_Staatstheater_am_G%C3%A4rtnerplatz_04.jpg/250px-Das_Staatstheater_am_G%C3%A4rtnerplatz_04.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Das_Staatstheater_am_Gärtnerplatz_04.jpg)\n\n[Staatstheater am Gärtnerplatz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Staatstheater_am_Gärtnerplatz \"Staatstheater am Gärtnerplatz\")\n\nThe National Theatre Munich is now home to the [Bavarian State Opera](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bavarian_State_Opera \"Bavarian State Opera\") and the [Bavarian State Orchestra](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bavarian_State_Orchestra \"Bavarian State Orchestra\"). Next door, the modern [Residenz Theatre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Residenz_Theatre \"Residenz Theatre\") was erected in the [former royal palace](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Munich_Residenz \"Munich Residenz\") that also houses the [Cuvilliés Theatre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cuvilliés_Theatre \"Cuvilliés Theatre\"). The [Staatstheater am Gärtnerplatz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Staatstheater_am_Gärtnerplatz \"Staatstheater am Gärtnerplatz\") is a state theater while another opera house, the [Prinzregententheater](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prinzregententheater \"Prinzregententheater\"), has become the home of the Bavarian Theater Academy and the [Munich Chamber Orchestra](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Munich_Chamber_Orchestra \"Munich Chamber Orchestra\").\n\nThe modern [Gasteig](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gasteig \"Gasteig\") centre houses the [Munich Philharmonic Orchestra](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Munich_Philharmonic \"Munich Philharmonic\"). The third orchestra in Munich with international importance is the [Bavarian Radio Symphony Orchestra](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bavarian_Radio_Symphony_Orchestra \"Bavarian Radio Symphony Orchestra\"). Its primary concert venue is the Herkulessaal in the former city royal residence, the [Munich Residenz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Munich_Residenz \"Munich Residenz\"). Many important conductors have been attracted by the city's orchestras, including [Felix Weingartner](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Felix_Weingartner \"Felix Weingartner\"), [Hans Pfitzner](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hans_Pfitzner \"Hans Pfitzner\"), [Hans Rosbaud](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hans_Rosbaud \"Hans Rosbaud\"), [Hans Knappertsbusch](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hans_Knappertsbusch \"Hans Knappertsbusch\"), [Sergiu Celibidache](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sergiu_Celibidache \"Sergiu Celibidache\"), [James Levine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Levine \"James Levine\"), [Christian Thielemann](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Christian_Thielemann \"Christian Thielemann\"), [Lorin Maazel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lorin_Maazel \"Lorin Maazel\"), [Rafael Kubelík](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rafael_Kubelík \"Rafael Kubelík\"), [Eugen Jochum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eugen_Jochum \"Eugen Jochum\"), Sir [Colin Davis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Colin_Davis \"Colin Davis\"), [Mariss Jansons](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mariss_Jansons \"Mariss Jansons\"), [Bruno Walter](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bruno_Walter \"Bruno Walter\"), [Georg Solti](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Georg_Solti \"Georg Solti\"), [Zubin Mehta](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zubin_Mehta \"Zubin Mehta\") and [Kent Nagano](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kent_Nagano \"Kent Nagano\"). A stage for shows, big events and musicals is the [Deutsche Theater](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Deutsches_Theater_München \"Deutsches Theater München\"). It is Germany's largest theatre for guest performances.[[148]](#cite_note-150)\n\n#### Pop and electronica\n\n[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Munich&action=edit&section=36 \"Edit section: Pop and electronica\")]\n\nIn the late-1960s Munich was the centre of [Krautrock](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Krautrock \"Krautrock\") in southern Germany, with bands such as [Amon Düül II](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amon_Düül_II \"Amon Düül II\"), [Embryo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Embryo_(band) \"Embryo (band)\") or [Popol Vuh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Popol_Vuh_(band) \"Popol Vuh (band)\") hailing from the city.[*[citation needed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed \"Wikipedia:Citation needed\")*]\n\nIn the 1970s, the [Musicland Studios](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Musicland_Studios \"Musicland Studios\") in Munich became a world-renowned recording studio, with bands such as the [Rolling Stones](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rolling_Stones \"Rolling Stones\"), [Led Zeppelin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Led_Zeppelin \"Led Zeppelin\"), [Deep Purple](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Deep_Purple \"Deep Purple\") and [Queen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Queen_(band) \"Queen (band)\") recording albums there.[[149]](#cite_note-mjunikdisco-151) Musicland Studios also played a role in the development of electronic music through the work of genre pioneer [Giorgio Moroder](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Giorgio_Moroder \"Giorgio Moroder\"), who invented [synth](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Synth \"Synth\") [disco](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Disco \"Disco\") and [electronic dance music](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Electronic_dance_music \"Electronic dance music\"), and the recordings of disco musician [Donna Summer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Donna_Summer \"Donna Summer\").[[149]](#cite_note-mjunikdisco-151)\n\nSeveral bands associated with the [Neue Deutsche Welle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Neue_Deutsche_Welle \"Neue Deutsche Welle\") of the late 1970s and 1980s were founded in Munich, including [Spider Murphy Gang](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Spider_Murphy_Gang \"Spider Murphy Gang\"), [Münchener Freiheit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Münchener_Freiheit_(band) \"Münchener Freiheit (band)\"), and [FSK](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FSK_(band) \"FSK (band)\").\n\nIn the late 1990s, [DJ Hell](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/DJ_Hell \"DJ Hell\") brought together international pioneers of the developing musical genre of [Electroclash](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Electroclash \"Electroclash\") through his [International DeeJay Gigolo Records](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_DeeJay_Gigolo_Records \"International DeeJay Gigolo Records\") label in Munich.[[149]](#cite_note-mjunikdisco-151)\n\nOther notable musicians from Munich include the singer-songwriter [Konstantin Wecker](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Konstantin_Wecker \"Konstantin Wecker\"), singer [Lou Bega](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lou_Bega \"Lou Bega\"), known for his cover of the song \"[Mambo No. 5](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mambo_No._5#Lou_Bega_version \"Mambo No. 5\")\", as well as the contemporary rock bands [Colour Haze](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Colour_Haze \"Colour Haze\") and [Sportfreunde Stiller](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sportfreunde_Stiller \"Sportfreunde Stiller\").[*[citation needed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed \"Wikipedia:Citation needed\")*]\n\nThe biggest club in the city by capacity, [Neuraum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Neuraum \"Neuraum\"), also regularly books [electronic dance music](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Electronic_dance_music \"Electronic dance music\") acts.[[150]](#cite_note-sz2010-152)\n\n### Theatre\n\n[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Munich&action=edit&section=37 \"Edit section: Theatre\")]\n\nThe [Munich Kammerspiele](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Munich_Kammerspiele \"Munich Kammerspiele\") is one of the most important German-language theatres.[*[citation needed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed \"Wikipedia:Citation needed\")*] Several notable playwrights have lived and worked in Munich, including [Christian Friedrich Hebbel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Christian_Friedrich_Hebbel \"Christian Friedrich Hebbel\"), [Henrik Ibsen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Henrik_Ibsen \"Henrik Ibsen\"), [Bertolt Brecht](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bertolt_Brecht \"Bertolt Brecht\"), and [Frank Wedekind](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Frank_Wedekind \"Frank Wedekind\").\n\nMunich was also the site of a vibrant [cabaret](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kabarett \"Kabarett\") performance scene during the Weimar era, epitomized by the short films and performances of [Karl Valentin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Karl_Valentin \"Karl Valentin\") and [Liesl Karlstadt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Liesl_Karlstadt \"Liesl Karlstadt\"). Their work is commemorated at the [Valentin-Karlstadt-Musäum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Valentin-Karlstadt-Musäum?action=edit&redlink=1 \"Valentin-Karlstadt-Musäum (page does not exist)\") in [Isartor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Isartor \"Isartor\").[[151]](#cite_note-153)\n\nThe [Circus Krone](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Circus_Krone \"Circus Krone\"), based in Munich, is one of the largest circuses in Europe.[[152]](#cite_note-154) It was the first and still is one of only a few in Western Europe to also occupy a [building](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Circus_Krone_Building \"Circus Krone Building\") of its own.[*[citation needed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed \"Wikipedia:Citation needed\")*]\n\n### Art and Literature\n\n[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Munich&action=edit&section=38 \"Edit section: Art and Literature\")]\n\nPainters and sculptors based in Munich in the 15th and 16th century included [Erasmus Grasser](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Erasmus_Grasser \"Erasmus Grasser\") and [Jan Polack](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jan_Polack \"Jan Polack\").\n\n#### Baroque and Rococo\n\n[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Munich&action=edit&section=39 \"Edit section: Baroque and Rococo\")]\n\nDuring the Baroque and Rococo eras, several sculptors, architects, and painters were based in Munich, including [Hans Krumpper](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hans_Krumpper \"Hans Krumpper\"), [Johann Baptist Zimmermann](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Johann_Baptist_Zimmermann \"Johann Baptist Zimmermann\"), [Cosmas Damian Asam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cosmas_Damian_Asam \"Cosmas Damian Asam\"), [Johann Michael Fischer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Johann_Michael_Fischer \"Johann Michael Fischer\"), [Egid Quirin Asam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Egid_Quirin_Asam \"Egid Quirin Asam\"), [François de Cuvilliés](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/François_de_Cuvilliés \"François de Cuvilliés\"), [Johann Baptist Straub](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Johann_Baptist_Straub \"Johann Baptist Straub\") and [Ignaz Günther](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ignaz_Günther \"Ignaz Günther\").\n\n#### Biedermeier\n\n[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Munich&action=edit&section=40 \"Edit section: Biedermeier\")]\n\nThe [Biedermeier](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Biedermeier \"Biedermeier\") era of the early 19th century was named after a character that regularly appeared in the satire magazine *[Fliegende Blätter](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fliegende_Blätter \"Fliegende Blätter\")*, which was published by [Adolf Kussmaul](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Adolf_Kussmaul \"Adolf Kussmaul\") and [Ludwig Eichrodt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ludwig_Eichrodt \"Ludwig Eichrodt\") in Munich between 1855 and 1857. The Biedermeier era painters [Ferdinand Georg Waldmüller](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ferdinand_Georg_Waldmüller \"Ferdinand Georg Waldmüller\"), [Moritz von Schwind](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Moritz_von_Schwind \"Moritz von Schwind\"), and [Carl Spitzweg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Carl_Spitzweg \"Carl Spitzweg\") are shown in the [Neue Pinakothek](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Neue_Pinakothek \"Neue Pinakothek\").[[153]](#cite_note-155)\n\nFurther artists of the Biedermeier era based in Munich include sculptor [Ludwig von Schwanthaler](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ludwig_Michael_Schwanthaler \"Ludwig Michael Schwanthaler\") and the painters [Carl Rottmann](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Carl_Rottmann \"Carl Rottmann\"), [Wilhelm von Kaulbach](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wilhelm_von_Kaulbach \"Wilhelm von Kaulbach\"), [Karl Piloty](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Karl_Piloty \"Karl Piloty\").[*[citation needed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed \"Wikipedia:Citation needed\")*]\n\n#### Prinzregentenzeit\n\n[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Munich&action=edit&section=41 \"Edit section: Prinzregentenzeit\")]\n\nCelebrity literary figures worked in Munich especially during the final decades of the Kingdom of Bavaria, the so-called *Prinzregentenzeit* from 1886 to 1912*,* under the reign of [Luitpold, Prince Regent of Bavaria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luitpold,_Prince_Regent_of_Bavaria \"Luitpold, Prince Regent of Bavaria\"). This includes the authors [Thomas Mann](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thomas_Mann \"Thomas Mann\"), [Heinrich Mann](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Heinrich_Mann \"Heinrich Mann\"), [Paul Johann Ludwig von Heyse](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paul_Johann_Ludwig_von_Heyse \"Paul Johann Ludwig von Heyse\"), [Rainer Maria Rilke](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rainer_Maria_Rilke \"Rainer Maria Rilke\"), [Ludwig Thoma](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ludwig_Thoma \"Ludwig Thoma\"), [Fanny zu Reventlow](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fanny_zu_Reventlow \"Fanny zu Reventlow\"), [Gustav Meyrink](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gustav_Meyrink \"Gustav Meyrink\"), and [Erich Mühsam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Erich_Mühsam \"Erich Mühsam\") as well as the playwrights [Oskar Panizza](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oskar_Panizza \"Oskar Panizza\"), [Frank Wedekind](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Frank_Wedekind \"Frank Wedekind\"), and [Max Halbe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Max_Halbe \"Max Halbe\"). [Thomas Mann](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thomas_Mann \"Thomas Mann\") wrote in his novella *Gladius Dei* about this period: \"München leuchtete\" (literally \"Munich shone\").\n\n[![](//thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/13/Vassily_Kandinsky%2C_1908_-_Houses_in_Munich.jpg/250px-Vassily_Kandinsky%2C_1908_-_Houses_in_Munich.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Vassily_Kandinsky,_1908_-_Houses_in_Munich.jpg)\n\nWassily Kandinsky's *Houses in Munich* (1908)\n\nAt the turn of the 20th century the neighbourhood of [Schwabing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Schwabing \"Schwabing\") became known as a bohemian cultural epicenter for both literature and the fine arts.[[154]](#cite_note-156) Central to Schwabing's bohemian scene were *Künstlerlokale* (*Artist's Cafés*) such as [Café Stefanie](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Café_Stefanie \"Café Stefanie\") or [Simpl](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Simpl_(Munich) \"Simpl (Munich)\"), whose liberal ways differed fundamentally from Munich's more traditional localities.[*[citation needed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed \"Wikipedia:Citation needed\")*] The Simpl, which survives to this day, was named after Munich's anti-authoritarian satirical magazine *[Simplicissimus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Simplicissimus \"Simplicissimus\")*, founded in 1896 by [Albert Langen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Albert_Langen \"Albert Langen\") and [Thomas Theodor Heine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thomas_Theodor_Heine \"Thomas Theodor Heine\").[*[citation needed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed \"Wikipedia:Citation needed\")*] [Vladimir Lenin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vladimir_Lenin \"Vladimir Lenin\") authored the pamphlet *[What Is to Be Done?](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/What_Is_to_Be_Done%3F \"What Is to Be Done?\")* while living in Schwabing.\n\nArtists of the late-19th and early-20th century based in Munich include [Franz von Lenbach](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Franz_von_Lenbach \"Franz von Lenbach\"), [Wilhelm Leibl](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wilhelm_Leibl \"Wilhelm Leibl\"), [Lovis Corinth](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lovis_Corinth \"Lovis Corinth\"), and [Franz Stuck](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Franz_Stuck \"Franz Stuck\").[*[citation needed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed \"Wikipedia:Citation needed\")*]\n\n#### Weimar Republic\n\n[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Munich&action=edit&section=42 \"Edit section: Weimar Republic\")]\n\n[![](//thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/81/Schrimpf_oskar_maria_graf.png/250px-Schrimpf_oskar_maria_graf.png?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Schrimpf_oskar_maria_graf.png)\n\nPortrait of Oskar Maria Graf by [Georg Schrimpf](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Georg_Schrimpf \"Georg Schrimpf\") (1927)\n\nMunich remained a centre of cultural life during the [Weimar Republic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Weimar_Republic \"Weimar Republic\"), with writers such as [Lion Feuchtwanger](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lion_Feuchtwanger \"Lion Feuchtwanger\"), [Bertolt Brecht](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bertolt_Brecht \"Bertolt Brecht\"), [Ricarda Huch](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ricarda_Huch \"Ricarda Huch\"), [Oskar Maria Graf](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oskar_Maria_Graf \"Oskar Maria Graf\"), [Annette Kolb](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Annette_Kolb \"Annette Kolb\"), [Ernst Toller](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ernst_Toller \"Ernst Toller\"), [Hugo Ball](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hugo_Ball \"Hugo Ball\"), and [Klaus Mann](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Klaus_Mann \"Klaus Mann\") living in the city.[[155]](#cite_note-157)\n\n#### Post-war literature\n\n[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Munich&action=edit&section=43 \"Edit section: Post-war literature\")]\n\nAfter World War II, Munich soon again became a focal point of the German literary scene and remains so to this day.[*[citation needed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed \"Wikipedia:Citation needed\")*] Writers such as [Wolfgang Koeppen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wolfgang_Koeppen \"Wolfgang Koeppen\"), [Erich Kästner](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Erich_Kästner \"Erich Kästner\"), [Eugen Roth](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eugen_Roth \"Eugen Roth\"), [Alfred Andersch](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alfred_Andersch \"Alfred Andersch\"), [Elfriede Jelinek](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Elfriede_Jelinek \"Elfriede Jelinek\"), [Hans Magnus Enzensberger](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hans_Magnus_Enzensberger \"Hans Magnus Enzensberger\"), [Michael Ende](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Michael_Ende \"Michael Ende\"), [Franz Xaver Kroetz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Franz_Xaver_Kroetz \"Franz Xaver Kroetz\"), [Gerhard Polt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gerhard_Polt \"Gerhard Polt\") and [Patrick Süskind](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Patrick_Süskind \"Patrick Süskind\") have called the city their home.[*[citation needed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed \"Wikipedia:Citation needed\")*]\n\n### Cinema\n\n[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Munich&action=edit&section=44 \"Edit section: Cinema\")]\n\nMunich has been home to many of the directors active in the [New German Cinema](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_German_Cinema \"New German Cinema\") movement, including [Rainer Werner Fassbinder](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rainer_Werner_Fassbinder \"Rainer Werner Fassbinder\"), [Werner Herzog](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Werner_Herzog \"Werner Herzog\"), [Edgar Reitz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Edgar_Reitz \"Edgar Reitz\") and [Herbert Achternbusch](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Herbert_Achternbusch \"Herbert Achternbusch\"). New German Cinema was a significant shift in post-war German filmmaking that rebuffed traditional funding structures and kitschy subject matter in favour of cinematic experimentation and political expression.[[156]](#cite_note-158)[[157]](#cite_note-159) In 1971, the [Filmverlag der Autoren](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Filmverlag_der_Autoren \"Filmverlag der Autoren\") was founded in Munich as an independent co-operative that produced and distributed the work of several of these directors.[[158]](#cite_note-160) Munich also served as the setting for many of Fassbinder's films, including *[Ali: Fear Eats the Soul](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ali:_Fear_Eats_the_Soul \"Ali: Fear Eats the Soul\")* (1974). Fassbinder and his \"clan\" of actors were also regulars at local sites, such as the Hotel [Deutsche Eiche](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Deutsche_Eiche?action=edit&redlink=1 \"Deutsche Eiche (page does not exist)\") near Gärtnerplatz.[*[citation needed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed \"Wikipedia:Citation needed\")*]\n\nIn 1919, the film producer and distributor [Bavaria Film](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bavaria_Film \"Bavaria Film\") was founded in [Grünwald](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Grünwald,_Bavaria \"Grünwald, Bavaria\") as Münchner Lichtspielkunst (Emelka), which developed into one of Europe's largest film studios.[*[citation needed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed \"Wikipedia:Citation needed\")*] Among the internationally well-known films produced at the associated Bavaria Studios are *[The Pleasure Garden](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Pleasure_Garden_(1925_film) \"The Pleasure Garden (1925 film)\")* (1925) by [Alfred Hitchcock](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alfred_Hitchcock \"Alfred Hitchcock\"), *[The Great Escape](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Great_Escape_(film) \"The Great Escape (film)\")* (1963) by [John Sturges](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_Sturges \"John Sturges\"), *[Paths of Glory](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paths_of_Glory \"Paths of Glory\")* (1957) by [Stanley Kubrick](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stanley_Kubrick \"Stanley Kubrick\"), *[Willy Wonka & the Chocolate Factory](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Willy_Wonka_&_the_Chocolate_Factory \"Willy Wonka & the Chocolate Factory\")* (1971) by [Mel Stuart](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mel_Stuart \"Mel Stuart\") and both *[Das Boot](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Das_Boot \"Das Boot\")* (1981) and *[The Neverending Story](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Neverending_Story_(film) \"The Neverending Story (film)\")* (1984) by [Wolfgang Petersen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wolfgang_Petersen \"Wolfgang Petersen\").\n\nOther significant companies based in the city include the motion picture film equipment manufacturer [Arri](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arri \"Arri\") and the film production company [Constantin Film](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Constantin_Film \"Constantin Film\"). The [University of Television and Film Munich](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_Television_and_Film_Munich \"University of Television and Film Munich\") (HFF) is also a publicly funded film school based in the city and the alma mater of [Maren Ade](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maren_Ade \"Maren Ade\"), [Wim Wenders](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wim_Wenders \"Wim Wenders\"), [Caroline Link](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Caroline_Link \"Caroline Link\"), and [Hito Steyerl](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hito_Steyerl \"Hito Steyerl\").\n\nMunich remains one of the centres of the German film and entertainment industry.[[159]](#cite_note-161) In addition to those above, directors such as [Billy Wilder](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Billy_Wilder \"Billy Wilder\"), [Orson Welles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Orson_Welles \"Orson Welles\"), [John Huston](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_Huston \"John Huston\"), [Ingmar Bergman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ingmar_Bergman \"Ingmar Bergman\"), [Claude Chabrol](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Claude_Chabrol \"Claude Chabrol\"), and [Fritz Umgelter](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fritz_Umgelter \"Fritz Umgelter\") and have made films in Munich.\n\n### Cuisine and culinary specialities\n\n[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Munich&action=edit&section=45 \"Edit section: Cuisine and culinary specialities\")]\n\n[![](//thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/6f/Weisswurst.jpg/250px-Weisswurst.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Weisswurst.jpg)\n\nWeißwurst with sweet mustard and a pretzel\n\nMunich cuisine is a part of the broader [Bavarian cuisine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bavarian_cuisine \"Bavarian cuisine\"). Munich [Weißwurst](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Weißwurst \"Weißwurst\") (\"white sausage\", *German: Münchner Weißwurst*) was invented in the city in 1857 and is a Munich speciality. Traditionally Weißwurst is served in pubs before noon and is served with [sweet mustard](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sweet_mustard \"Sweet mustard\") and freshly baked [pretzels](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pretzel \"Pretzel\").\n\nMunich has 11 restaurants that have been awarded one or more [Michelin Guide](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Michelin_Guide \"Michelin Guide\") stars in 2021.[[160]](#cite_note-MichelinGuide-2021-162)\n\nIn 1971 [Eckart Witzigmann](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eckart_Witzigmann \"Eckart Witzigmann\") teamed up with a Munich building contractor to finance and open the *Tantris* restaurant in Schwabing. Witzigmann is credited for starting the German *Küchenwunder* (*kitchen wonder*).[[161]](#cite_note-163)\n\n#### Beers and breweries\n\n[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Munich&action=edit&section=46 \"Edit section: Beers and breweries\")]\n\n[![](//thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/04/Biergarten_at_Night_2.JPG/250px-Biergarten_at_Night_2.JPG?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Biergarten_at_Night_2.JPG)\n\nBeer garden in Munich\n\nMunich is known for its breweries and [Weissbier](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Weissbier \"Weissbier\") (*wheat beer*). [Helles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Helles \"Helles\"), a [pale lager](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pale_lager \"Pale lager\") with a translucent gold color, is the most popular contemporary Munich beer. Helles has largely replaced Munich's dark beer, known as [Dunkel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dunkel \"Dunkel\"), which gets its color from roasted malt. It was the typical beer in Munich in the 19th century. Starkbier is the strongest Munich beer, with a high alcohol content of 6%–9%. It is dark amber in color and has a heavy malty taste. The beer served at [Oktoberfest](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oktoberfest \"Oktoberfest\") is a special type of beer with a higher alcohol content.\n\nWirtshäuser are traditional Bavarian pubs, many of which also have small outside areas. Biergärten (*[beer gardens](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beer_garden \"Beer garden\")*) are a popular fixture in Munich's gastronomic landscape. They are central to the city's culture, and are an overt melting pot for members of all walks of life, regardless of social class. There are many smaller beer gardens, but some beer gardens have thousands of seats. Large beer gardens can be found in the [Englischer Garten](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Englischer_Garten \"Englischer Garten\"), on the Nockherberg, and in the Hirschgarten.\n\nThe six main breweries in Munich are [Augustiner-Bräu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Augustiner-Bräu \"Augustiner-Bräu\"), [Hacker-Pschorr Brewery](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hacker-Pschorr_Brewery \"Hacker-Pschorr Brewery\"), [Hofbräuhaus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hofbräuhaus \"Hofbräuhaus\"), [Löwenbräu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Löwenbräu \"Löwenbräu\"), [Paulaner](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paulaner \"Paulaner\"), and [Spaten-Franziskaner-Bräu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Spaten-Franziskaner-Bräu \"Spaten-Franziskaner-Bräu\"). Smaller breweries are becoming more prevalent in Munich.\n\n### Festivals\n\n[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Munich&action=edit&section=47 \"Edit section: Festivals\")]\n\n[![](//thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/e4/Hippodrom_Zelt_Oktoberfest.jpg/250px-Hippodrom_Zelt_Oktoberfest.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Hippodrom_Zelt_Oktoberfest.jpg)\n\nThe [Oktoberfest](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oktoberfest \"Oktoberfest\") is the world's largest [beer festival](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beer_festival \"Beer festival\"), attracting around seven million visitors every year.\n\n#### Coopers' Dance\n\n[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Munich&action=edit&section=48 \"Edit section: Coopers' Dance\")]\n\n[![](//thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/3f/Sch%C3%A4fflertanz_2012-05.JPG/250px-Sch%C3%A4fflertanz_2012-05.JPG?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Schäfflertanz_2012-05.JPG)\n\nSchäfflertanz in [Neuhausen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Neuhausen-Nymphenburg \"Neuhausen-Nymphenburg\"), 2012\n\nThe [Coopers' Dance](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coopers'_Dance \"Coopers' Dance\") (German: *Schäfflertanz*) is a [guild](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Medieval_guild \"Medieval guild\") dance of [coopers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cooper_(profession) \"Cooper (profession)\") originally started in Munich. Beginning in the early 1800s the custom spread via the travels of [journeymen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Journeyman \"Journeyman\"), and it is now a common tradition throughout the [Old Bavaria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Old_Bavaria \"Old Bavaria\") region. The dance was supposed to be held every seven years.[[162]](#cite_note-myth-164)\n\n#### Strong Beer Festival\n\n[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Munich&action=edit&section=49 \"Edit section: Strong Beer Festival\")]\n\nMunich is home to the famous [Nockherberg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nockherberg \"Nockherberg\") Strong Beer Festival ([German](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/German_language \"German language\"): *Starkbierfest*) during the Lenten fasting period (usually in March). Its origins go back to the 17th/18th century, but has become popular when the festivities were first televised in the 1980s. The festival includes comical speeches and a mini-musical in which numerous German politicians are parodied by look-alike actors.[[163]](#cite_note-165)\n\n#### Frühlingsfest\n\n[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Munich&action=edit&section=50 \"Edit section: Frühlingsfest\")]\n\nHeld for two weeks at the [Theresienwiese](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Theresienwiese \"Theresienwiese\") from the end of April to the beginning of May, celebrating and serving the new local spring beers.[[164]](#cite_note-166)\n\n#### Auer Dult\n\n[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Munich&action=edit&section=51 \"Edit section: Auer Dult\")]\n\nMain article: [Auer Dult](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Auer_Dult \"Auer Dult\")\n\nA regular event combining a [market](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marketplace \"Marketplace\") and a German style [folk festival](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Volksfest \"Volksfest\") on the [Mariahilfplatz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mariahilfplatz \"Mariahilfplatz\"). The Auer Dult can be up to 300 stalls, selling handmade crafts, [household goods](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Household_goods \"Household goods\"), and [local foods](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Local_food \"Local food\").[[165]](#cite_note-167)\n\n#### Kocherlball\n\n[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Munich&action=edit&section=52 \"Edit section: Kocherlball\")]\n\nMunich's Kocherlball (*Cooks' Ball*) is an annual event to commemorate all servants, ranging from kitchenhands to cooks. The tradition started in the 19th century.[[166]](#cite_note-168)\n\n#### Tollwood\n\n[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Munich&action=edit&section=53 \"Edit section: Tollwood\")]\n\n[![](//thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/fd/Tollwood_Winterfestival_Munich_2010_b.jpg/250px-Tollwood_Winterfestival_Munich_2010_b.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Tollwood_Winterfestival_Munich_2010_b.jpg)\n\n[Tollwood Festival](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tollwood_Festival \"Tollwood Festival\")\n\nUsually held annually in July and December at Olympia Park. The [Tollwood Festival](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tollwood_Festival \"Tollwood Festival\") showcases fine and performing arts with live music, and several lanes of booths selling handmade crafts, as well as [organic food](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Organic_food \"Organic food\"), mostly [fusion cuisine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fusion_cuisine \"Fusion cuisine\").[[167]](#cite_note-169)\n\n#### Oktoberfest\n\n[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Munich&action=edit&section=54 \"Edit section: Oktoberfest\")]\n\nAt [Theresienwiese](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Theresienwiese \"Theresienwiese\"), Munich's [Oktoberfest](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oktoberfest \"Oktoberfest\"), the largest [beer festival](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beer_festival \"Beer festival\") and *[Volksfest](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Volksfest \"Volksfest\")* in the world, runs for 16–18 days from the end of September through early October. In the last 200 years the festival has grown to span 85 acres and now welcomes over six million visitors every year. Beer is served from the six major Munich [breweries](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brewery \"Brewery\"): [Augustiner-Bräu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Augustiner-Bräu \"Augustiner-Bräu\"), [Hacker-Pschorr Brewery](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hacker-Pschorr_Brewery \"Hacker-Pschorr Brewery\"), [Löwenbräu Brewery](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Löwenbräu_Brewery \"Löwenbräu Brewery\"), [Paulaner Brewery](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paulaner_Brewery \"Paulaner Brewery\"), [Spaten-Franziskaner-Bräu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Spaten-Franziskaner-Bräu \"Spaten-Franziskaner-Bräu\"), and [Staatliches Hofbräuhaus in München](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Staatliches_Hofbräuhaus_in_München \"Staatliches Hofbräuhaus in München\").[*[citation needed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed \"Wikipedia:Citation needed\")*] Food must be bought in each tent.[[168]](#cite_note-170)\n\n#### Christkindlmarkt\n\n[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Munich&action=edit&section=55 \"Edit section: Christkindlmarkt\")]\n\nThe Munich [Christkindlmarkt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Christkindlmarkt \"Christkindlmarkt\") started to evolve in the 14th century. The German Christkindlmarkt reached the desired accomplishment[*[clarification needed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia:Please_clarify \"Wikipedia:Please clarify\")*] in the 17th century in [Nuremberg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nuremberg \"Nuremberg\").[[169]](#cite_note-171)\n\n### Nightlife\n\n[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Munich&action=edit&section=56 \"Edit section: Nightlife\")]\n\n[Nightlife](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nightlife \"Nightlife\") in Munich is located mostly in the boroughs [Ludwigsvorstadt-Isarvorstadt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ludwigsvorstadt-Isarvorstadt \"Ludwigsvorstadt-Isarvorstadt\"), [Maxvorstadt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maxvorstadt \"Maxvorstadt\"), [Au-Haidhausen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Au-Haidhausen \"Au-Haidhausen\"), [Berg am Laim](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Berg_am_Laim \"Berg am Laim\") and [Sendling](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sendling \"Sendling\"). Between [Sendlinger Tor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sendlinger_Tor \"Sendlinger Tor\") and Maximiliansplatz, on the edge of the central [Altstadt-Lehel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Altstadt-Lehel \"Altstadt-Lehel\") district, there is also the so-called Feierbanane (party banana), a roughly banana-shaped unofficial party zone spanning 1.3 km (0.8 mi) along Sonnenstraße, characterized by a high concentration of clubs, bars and restaurants, which became the center of Munich's nightlife in the mid-2000s.[[170]](#cite_note-sueddeutsche_20130706-172)\n\n[![](//thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/dd/Bahnw%C3%A4rter_Thiel_Nightclub_Munich_Subway_Floor_1.jpg/250px-Bahnw%C3%A4rter_Thiel_Nightclub_Munich_Subway_Floor_1.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Bahnwärter_Thiel_Nightclub_Munich_Subway_Floor_1.jpg)\n\n[Bahnwärter Thiel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bahnwärter_Thiel_(club) \"Bahnwärter Thiel (club)\")\n\nIn the 1960s and 1970s, [Schwabing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Schwabing \"Schwabing\") was considered a center of nightlife in Germany, with internationally known clubs such as [Big Apple](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Big_Apple_(club) \"Big Apple (club)\"), PN hit-house, Domicile, Hot Club, Piper Club, Tiffany, Germany's first large-scale discotheque [Blow Up](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Blow_Up_(club) \"Blow Up (club)\") and the underwater nightclub [Yellow Submarine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yellow_Submarine_(club) \"Yellow Submarine (club)\"),[[149]](#cite_note-mjunikdisco-151)[[171]](#cite_note-ertl-173)[[172]](#cite_note-mitvergnuegen_schauberger-174) and Munich has been called \"New York's big disco sister\" in this context.[[149]](#cite_note-mjunikdisco-151)[[173]](#cite_note-abendzeitung_20081121-175) Bars in the Schwabing district of this era include, among many others, [Schwabinger 7](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Schwabinger_7 \"Schwabinger 7\") and Schwabinger Podium. Since the 1980s, however, Schwabing has lost much of its nightlife activity due to [gentrification](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gentrification \"Gentrification\") and the resulting high rents, and the formerly wild artists' and students' quarter developed into one of the city's most coveted and expensive residential districts, attracting affluent citizens with little interest in partying.[[174]](#cite_note-mitvergnuegen_nisslmüller-176)\n\nBeginning in the 1960s, the Rosa Viertel (pink quarter) developed in the [Glockenbachviertel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ludwigsvorstadt-Isarvorstadt#Glockenbachviertel \"Ludwigsvorstadt-Isarvorstadt\") and around Gärtnerplatz, which in the 1980s made Munich \"one of the four gayest metropolises in the world\" along with San Francisco, New York City and Amsterdam.[[175]](#cite_note-stankiewitz-177) In particular, the area around [Müllerstraße](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Müllerstraße \"Müllerstraße\") and Hans-Sachs-Straße was characterized by numerous gay bars and nightclubs. One of them was the [travesty](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Travesti_(theatre) \"Travesti (theatre)\") nightclub Old Mrs. Henderson, where [Freddie Mercury](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Freddie_Mercury \"Freddie Mercury\"), who lived in Munich from 1979 to 1985, filmed the music video for the song \"[Living on My Own](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Living_on_My_Own \"Living on My Own\")\" at his 39th birthday party.[[175]](#cite_note-stankiewitz-177)[[172]](#cite_note-mitvergnuegen_schauberger-174)[[176]](#cite_note-BR_20211004-178)\n\nMunich hosted several *Love Parades* and *Mayday Party* [rave](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rave \"Rave\") events throughout the 1990s. Munich continues to rave, the local youth scenes are active.[[177]](#cite_note-179)\n\nSince the mid-1990s, the Kunstpark Ost and its successor Kultfabrik, a former industrial complex that was converted to a large party area near [München Ostbahnhof](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Munich_East_station \"Munich East station\") in [Berg am Laim](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Berg_am_Laim \"Berg am Laim\"), hosted more than 30 clubs and was especially popular among younger people from the metropolitan area surrounding Munich and tourists.[[175]](#cite_note-stankiewitz-177)[[178]](#cite_note-munichfoundcom-180) The Kultfabrik was closed at the end of the year 2015 to convert the area into a residential and office area. Apart from the Kultfarbik and the smaller Optimolwerke, there is a wide variety of establishments in the urban parts of nearby [Haidhausen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Haidhausen_(Munich) \"Haidhausen (Munich)\"). Before the Kunstpark Ost, there had already been an accumulation of internationally known nightclubs in the remains of the abandoned former [Munich-Riem Airport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Munich-Riem_Airport#Reuse \"Munich-Riem Airport\").[[149]](#cite_note-mjunikdisco-151)[[179]](#cite_note-mitvergnuegen_022017-181)[[180]](#cite_note-ard_072022-182)\n\n[![](//thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/9a/Blitz_Club_Munich.jpg/250px-Blitz_Club_Munich.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Blitz_Club_Munich.jpg)\n\nBlitz Club on Museumsinsel\n\nMunich nightlife tends to change dramatically and quickly. Establishments open and close every year, and due to gentrification and the overheated housing market many survive only a few years, while others last longer. Beyond the already mentioned venues of the 1960s and 1970s, nightclubs with international recognition in recent history included Tanzlokal Größenwahn, [The Atomic Café](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Atomic_Café_(club) \"The Atomic Café (club)\") and the techno clubs [Babalu Club](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Babalu_Club \"Babalu Club\"), [Ultraschall](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ultraschall \"Ultraschall\"), [KW – Das Heizkraftwerk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/KW_–_Das_Heizkraftwerk \"KW – Das Heizkraftwerk\"), [Natraj Temple](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Natraj_Temple \"Natraj Temple\"), [MMA Club (Mixed Munich Arts)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MMA_Club \"MMA Club\"), Die Registratur and Bob Beaman.[[181]](#cite_note-zeitmagazin-183) From 1995 to 2001, Munich was also home to the [Union Move](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Union_Move \"Union Move\"), one of the largest [technoparades](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Technoparade \"Technoparade\") in Germany.[[171]](#cite_note-ertl-173)\n\nMunich has the highest density of music venues of any German city, followed by Hamburg, Cologne and Berlin.[[182]](#cite_note-fazemag_20210920-184)[[183]](#cite_note-clubstudy_2021-185) Within the city's limits are more than 100 nightclubs and thousands of bars and restaurants.[[184]](#cite_note-Munich_bars-186)[[185]](#cite_note-Munich_nightclubs-187)\n\nPopular techno nightclubs are [Blitz Club](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Blitz_Club \"Blitz Club\"), Harry Klein, Rote Sonne, [Bahnwärter Thiel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bahnwärter_Thiel_(club) \"Bahnwärter Thiel (club)\"), Pimpernel, Charlie, Palais and Pathos.[[186]](#cite_note-sueddeutsche_20161016-188)[[187]](#cite_note-theclubmap-189) Popular mixed music clubs are Call me Drella, Wannda Circus, Tonhalle, Backstage, Muffathalle, Ampere, Pacha, P1, [Zenith](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zenith_(building) \"Zenith (building)\"), Minna Thiel and the party ship [Alte Utting](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alte_Utting \"Alte Utting\").\n\n## Sports\n\n[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Munich&action=edit&section=57 \"Edit section: Sports\")]\n\nMain article: [Sport in Munich](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sport_in_Munich \"Sport in Munich\")\n\n### Football\n\n[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Munich&action=edit&section=58 \"Edit section: Football\")]\n\nMain article: [Football in Munich](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Football_in_Munich \"Football in Munich\")\n\n[![](//thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/e9/Allianz_Arena_2008-02-09.jpg/250px-Allianz_Arena_2008-02-09.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Allianz_Arena_2008-02-09.jpg)\n\n[Allianz Arena](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Allianz_Arena \"Allianz Arena\") is the second-largest stadium in Germany and the home stadium of [FC Bayern Munich](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FC_Bayern_Munich \"FC Bayern Munich\").\n\nMunich is home to several professional Association football teams including the [FC Bayern Munich](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FC_Bayern_Munich \"FC Bayern Munich\"). Other notable clubs include [1860 Munich](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/TSV_1860_Munich \"TSV 1860 Munich\"), who currently play in the [3. Liga](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/3._Liga \"3. Liga\"). Noticeably, [FC Bayern Munich](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FC_Bayern_Munich \"FC Bayern Munich\") is the most successful club in Germany and it is also very reputed across Europe and the world. Munich hosted matches in the [2006 FIFA World Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2006_FIFA_World_Cup \"2006 FIFA World Cup\").[[188]](#cite_note-190)\n\n### Basketball\n\n[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Munich&action=edit&section=59 \"Edit section: Basketball\")]\n\n[FC Bayern Munich Basketball](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FC_Bayern_Munich_(basketball) \"FC Bayern Munich (basketball)\") is currently playing in the Beko Basket Bundesliga. The city hosted the final stages of the FIBA [EuroBasket 1993](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/EuroBasket_1993 \"EuroBasket 1993\"), where the [German national basketball team](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/German_national_basketball_team \"German national basketball team\") won the gold medal.\n\n### Ice hockey\n\n[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Munich&action=edit&section=60 \"Edit section: Ice hockey\")]\n\nThe city's ice hockey club is [EHC Red Bull München](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/EHC_Red_Bull_München \"EHC Red Bull München\") who play in the [Deutsche Eishockey Liga](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Deutsche_Eishockey_Liga \"Deutsche Eishockey Liga\"). The team has won four DEL Championships, in 2016, 2017, 2018 and 2023.\n\n### Olympics\n\n[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Munich&action=edit&section=61 \"Edit section: Olympics\")]\n\n[![](//thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/d1/M%C3%BCnchen_-_Olympische_Bauten.jpg/250px-M%C3%BCnchen_-_Olympische_Bauten.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:München_-_Olympische_Bauten.jpg)\n\nOlympiasee in the [Olympiapark](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Olympiapark \"Olympiapark\")\n\nMunich hosted the [1972 Summer Olympics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1972_Summer_Olympics \"1972 Summer Olympics\"); the [Munich massacre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Munich_massacre \"Munich massacre\") took place in the [Olympic village](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Olympic_Village,_Munich \"Olympic Village, Munich\"). It was one of the host cities for the [2006 Football World Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2006_FIFA_World_Cup \"2006 FIFA World Cup\"), which was not held in Munich's [Olympic Stadium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Olympic_Stadium_(Munich) \"Olympic Stadium (Munich)\"), but in a new [football specific stadium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Soccer-specific_stadium \"Soccer-specific stadium\"), the [Allianz Arena](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Allianz_Arena \"Allianz Arena\"). Munich bid to host the [2018 Winter Olympic Games](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2018_Winter_Olympic_Games \"2018 Winter Olympic Games\"), but lost to [Pyeongchang](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pyeongchang_County \"Pyeongchang County\").[[189]](#cite_note-191)\n\nIn October 2025, Munich citizens voted heavily (66%)[[190]](#cite_note-192) in favor of their city's bid to host the 2036, 2040, or 2044 Olympic Games. The pro-bid campaign[[191]](#cite_note-193) focused on ecological and sustainable solutions, social housing, and leveraging existing infrastructure.\n\n### Road running\n\n[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Munich&action=edit&section=62 \"Edit section: Road running\")]\n\nRegular annual road running events in Munich are the [Munich Marathon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Munich_Marathon \"Munich Marathon\") in October, the Stadtlauf end of June, the company run B2Run in July, the New Year's Run on 31 December, the [Spartan Race](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Spartan_Race \"Spartan Race\") Sprint, the Olympia Alm Crosslauf and the Bestzeitenmarathon.\n\n### Swimming\n\n[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Munich&action=edit&section=63 \"Edit section: Swimming\")]\n\nPublic sporting facilities in Munich include ten indoor swimming pools[[192]](#cite_note-publicindoorpools-194) and eight outdoor swimming pools,[[193]](#cite_note-publicoutdoorpools-195) which are operated by the [Munich City Utilities (SWM)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stadtwerke_München \"Stadtwerke München\") communal company.[[194]](#cite_note-SWM-196) Popular indoor swimming pools include the [Olympia Schwimmhalle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Olympia_Schwimmhalle \"Olympia Schwimmhalle\") of the [1972 Summer Olympics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1972_Summer_Olympics \"1972 Summer Olympics\"), the wave pool Cosimawellenbad, as well as the Müllersches Volksbad which was built in 1901. Further, swimming within Munich's city limits is also possible in several artificial lakes such as for example the [Riemer See](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Riemer_See \"Riemer See\") or the [Langwieder lake district](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Langwieder_lake_district \"Langwieder lake district\").[[195]](#cite_note-lakesinmunich-197)\n\n### River surfing\n\n[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Munich&action=edit&section=64 \"Edit section: River surfing\")]\n\n[River surfing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/River_surfing \"River surfing\") is a popular sport in Munich. The Flosskanal wave in the south of Munich is less challenging. A well visited surfing spot for experienced surfers is the [Eisbach](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eisbach_(Isar) \"Eisbach (Isar)\") standing wave, where the annual Munich Surf Open is celebrated on the last Saturday of July.[[196]](#cite_note-198)\n\n## Economy\n\n[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Munich&action=edit&section=65 \"Edit section: Economy\")]\n\n[![](//thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/3c/4_Cilindros%2C_M%C3%BAnich%2C_Alemania%2C_2013-02-11%2C_DD_02.JPG/250px-4_Cilindros%2C_M%C3%BAnich%2C_Alemania%2C_2013-02-11%2C_DD_02.JPG?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:4_Cilindros,_Múnich,_Alemania,_2013-02-11,_DD_02.JPG)\n\n[BMW Headquarters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BMW_Headquarters \"BMW Headquarters\") building (one of the few buildings that has been built from the top to the bottom) and the bowl-shaped [BMW Museum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BMW_Museum \"BMW Museum\")\n\n [![](//thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/a5/Hypo-Haus.JPG/250px-Hypo-Haus.JPG?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Hypo-Haus.JPG)\n\nThe [HypoVereinsbank](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/HypoVereinsbank \"HypoVereinsbank\") tower\n\nMunich has the strongest economy of any German city according to a study[[197]](#cite_note-Icm-muenchen.de-199) and the lowest unemployment rate (5.4% in July 2026[[198]](#cite_note-200)) of any German city of more than a million people (the others being [Berlin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Berlin \"Berlin\"), Hamburg and [Cologne](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cologne \"Cologne\")).[[199]](#cite_note-201)[[200]](#cite_note-202) Munich [ranks third](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_German_cities_by_GDP \"List of German cities by GDP\") on the list of German cities by gross domestic product (GDP). In addition, it is one of the most attractive business locations in Germany.[[197]](#cite_note-Icm-muenchen.de-199) The city is also the economic centre of [southern Germany](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Southern_Germany \"Southern Germany\"). Munich topped the ranking of the magazine *Capital* in February 2005 for the economic prospects between 2002 and 2011 in 60 German cities.\n\nMunich is a financial, business and commercial center and [global city](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Global_city \"Global city\") that holds the headquarters of many companies. This includes more companies listed by the [DAX](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/DAX \"DAX\") than any other German city, as well as the German or European headquarters of many foreign companies such as [McDonald's](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/McDonald's \"McDonald's\") and [Microsoft](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Microsoft \"Microsoft\"). One of the best-known newly established Munich companies is [Flixbus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Flixbus \"Flixbus\").\n\n### Manufacturing\n\n[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Munich&action=edit&section=66 \"Edit section: Manufacturing\")]\n\nMunich holds the headquarters of [Siemens AG](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Siemens \"Siemens\") (electronics), [BMW](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BMW \"BMW\") (car), [Traton](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Traton \"Traton\") (truck manufacturer, engineering), [MTU Aero Engines](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MTU_Aero_Engines \"MTU Aero Engines\") (aircraft engine manufacturer), [Linde](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Linde_Group \"The Linde Group\") (gases) and [Rohde & Schwarz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rohde_&_Schwarz \"Rohde & Schwarz\") ([electronics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Electronics \"Electronics\")). Among German cities with more than 500,000 inhabitants, purchasing power is highest in Munich (€26,648 per inhabitant) as of 2007[[update]](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Munich&action=edit).[[201]](#cite_note-203) In 2006, Munich blue-collar workers enjoyed an average hourly wage of €18.62 (ca. $20).[[202]](#cite_note-204)\n\nThe breakdown by cities proper (not metropolitan areas) of Global 500 cities listed Munich in 8th position in 2009.[[203]](#cite_note-205) Munich is also a centre for [biotechnology](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Biotechnology \"Biotechnology\"), software and other [service industries](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tertiary_sector_of_the_economy \"Tertiary sector of the economy\"). Furthermore, Munich is the home of the headquarters of many other large companies such as the [injection moulding machine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Injection_moulding_machine \"Injection moulding machine\") manufacturer [Krauss-Maffei](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Krauss-Maffei \"Krauss-Maffei\"), and its arms manufacturing branch [Krauss-Maffei & Wegmann](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Krauss-Maffei_Wegmann \"Krauss-Maffei Wegmann\"), the camera and lighting manufacturer [Arri](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arri \"Arri\"), the semiconductor firm [Infineon Technologies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Infineon_Technologies \"Infineon Technologies\") (headquartered in the suburban town of [Neubiberg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Neubiberg \"Neubiberg\")), lighting giant [Osram](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Osram \"Osram\"), as well as the German or European headquarters of many foreign companies such as [Microsoft](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Microsoft \"Microsoft\").\n\n### Finance\n\n[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Munich&action=edit&section=67 \"Edit section: Finance\")]\n\nMunich has significance as a [financial centre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Munich's_financial_community \"Munich's financial community\") (second only to [Frankfurt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Frankfurt_am_Main \"Frankfurt am Main\")), being home of [HypoVereinsbank](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/HypoVereinsbank \"HypoVereinsbank\") and the [Bayerische Landesbank](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bayerische_Landesbank \"Bayerische Landesbank\"). It outranks [Frankfurt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Frankfurt_am_Main \"Frankfurt am Main\") though as home of insurance companies such as [Allianz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Allianz \"Allianz\") (insurance) and [Munich Re](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Munich_Re \"Munich Re\") ([re-insurance](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Reinsurance \"Reinsurance\")).[[204]](#cite_note-206)\n\n### Media\n\n[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Munich&action=edit&section=68 \"Edit section: Media\")]\n\nMunich is the largest publishing city in Europe[[205]](#cite_note-207) and home to the *[Süddeutsche Zeitung](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Süddeutsche_Zeitung \"Süddeutsche Zeitung\")*, one of Germany's biggest daily newspapers. The city is also the location of the programming headquarters of Germany's largest public broadcasting network, [ARD](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ARD_(broadcaster) \"ARD (broadcaster)\"), while the largest commercial network, [Pro7-Sat1 Media AG](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ProSieben \"ProSieben\"), is headquartered in the suburb of [Unterföhring](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Unterföhring \"Unterföhring\"). The headquarters of the German branch of [Random House](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Random_House \"Random House\"), the world's largest publishing house, and of [Burda publishing group](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hubert_Burda_Media \"Hubert Burda Media\") are also in Munich.\n\nThe [Bavaria Film Studios](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bavaria_Film_Studios \"Bavaria Film Studios\") are located in the suburb of [Grünwald](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Grünwald,_Bavaria \"Grünwald, Bavaria\"). They are one of Europe's biggest film production studios.[[206]](#cite_note-208)\n\n### Technology\n\n[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Munich&action=edit&section=69 \"Edit section: Technology\")]\n\n* [Check24](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Check24 \"Check24\"), an online price comparison company founded in 1999[[207]](#cite_note-209)\n* [Celonis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Celonis \"Celonis\"), a process-mining software company with headquarters in Munich and New York[[208]](#cite_note-210)\n* [Personio](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Personio \"Personio\"), a human-resources software company founded in 2015[[209]](#cite_note-211)\n* [Scout24](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Scout24 \"Scout24\"), an online marketplace company operating the real-estate platform ImmoScout24\n* [Teamwire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Teamwire \"Teamwire\"), a business messaging software company founded in 2010\n* [Experteer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Experteer \"Experteer\"), an online recruitment platform for professionals and executives, founded in 2005\n\n## Education\n\n[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Munich&action=edit&section=70 \"Edit section: Education\")]\n\n### Colleges and universities\n\n[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Munich&action=edit&section=71 \"Edit section: Colleges and universities\")]\n\n[![](//thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/18/M%C3%BCnchen%2C_Geschwister-Scholl-Platz-1.jpg/250px-M%C3%BCnchen%2C_Geschwister-Scholl-Platz-1.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:München,_Geschwister-Scholl-Platz-1.jpg)\n\nMain building of [LMU Munich](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/LMU_Munich \"LMU Munich\")\n\n [![](//thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/14/TU_M%C3%BCnchen_GO-1.jpg/250px-TU_M%C3%BCnchen_GO-1.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:TU_München_GO-1.jpg)\n\nMain building of the [Technical University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Technical_University_of_Munich \"Technical University of Munich\")\n\nMunich is a leading location for science and research with a long list of Nobel Prize laureates from [Wilhelm Röntgen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wilhelm_Röntgen \"Wilhelm Röntgen\") in 1901 to [Theodor W. Hänsch](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Theodor_W._Hänsch \"Theodor W. Hänsch\") in 2005.\n\nLMU Munich[[210]](#cite_note-212) and the Technical University of Munich (TUM),[[211]](#cite_note-213) were two of the first three German universities to be awarded the title *elite university* by a selection committee composed of academics and members of the Ministries of Education and Research of the Federation and the German states (Länder).\n\n* [LMU Munich](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/LMU_Munich \"LMU Munich\"), founded in 1472 in [Ingolstadt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ingolstadt \"Ingolstadt\"), moved to Munich in 1826\n* [Technical University of Munich](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Technical_University_of_Munich \"Technical University of Munich\") (TUM), founded in 1868\n* [Akademie der Bildenden Künste München](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Academy_of_Fine_Arts,_Munich \"Academy of Fine Arts, Munich\"), founded in 1808\n* [University of the Bundeswehr Munich](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_the_Bundeswehr_Munich \"University of the Bundeswehr Munich\"), founded in 1973 (located in [Neubiberg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Neubiberg
