\"Neubiberg\"))\n* [Deutsche Journalistenschule](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Deutsche_Journalistenschule \"Deutsche Journalistenschule\"), founded in 1959\n* [Bayerische Akademie für Außenwirtschaft](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bayerische_Akademie_für_Außenwirtschaft \"Bayerische Akademie für Außenwirtschaft\"), founded in 1989\n* [Hochschule für Musik und Theater München](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hochschule_für_Musik_und_Theater_München \"Hochschule für Musik und Theater München\"), founded in 1830\n* [International Max Planck Research School for Molecular and Cellular Life Sciences](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_Max_Planck_Research_School_for_Molecular_and_Cellular_Life_Sciences \"International Max Planck Research School for Molecular and Cellular Life Sciences\"), founded in 2005\n* [International School of Management, Germany](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_School_of_Management,_Germany \"International School of Management, Germany\"), founded in 1990\n* [Katholische Stiftungsfachhochschule München](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Katholische_Stiftungsfachhochschule_München?action=edit&redlink=1 \"Katholische Stiftungsfachhochschule München (page does not exist)\"), founded in 1971\n* [Munich Business School](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Munich_Business_School \"Munich Business School\") (MBS), founded in 1991\n* [Munich Intellectual Property Law Center](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Munich_Intellectual_Property_Law_Center \"Munich Intellectual Property Law Center\") (MIPLC), founded in 2003\n* [Munich School of Philosophy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Munich_School_of_Philosophy \"Munich School of Philosophy\"), founded in 1925 in [Pullach](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pullach \"Pullach\"), moved to Munich in 1971\n* [Munich School of Political Science](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Munich_School_of_Political_Science \"Munich School of Political Science\"), founded in 1950\n* [Munich University of Applied Sciences](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Munich_University_of_Applied_Sciences \"Munich University of Applied Sciences\") (HM), founded in 1971\n* [New European College](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_European_College \"New European College\"), founded in 2014\n* [Ukrainian Free University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ukrainian_Free_University \"Ukrainian Free University\"), founded in 1921 (from 1945 – in Munich)\n* [University of Television and Film Munich](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_Television_and_Film_Munich \"University of Television and Film Munich\") (*Hochschule für Fernsehen und Film*), founded in 1966\n\n### Primary and secondary schools\n\n[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Munich&action=edit&section=72 \"Edit section: Primary and secondary schools\")]\n\nNotable Gymnasien in Munich include the [Maria-Theresia-Gymnasium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maria-Theresia-Gymnasium \"Maria-Theresia-Gymnasium\"), the [Luitpold Gymnasium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luitpold_Gymnasium \"Luitpold Gymnasium\"), the [Wilhelmsgymnasium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wilhelmsgymnasium_(Munich) \"Wilhelmsgymnasium (Munich)\"), as well as the [Wittelsbacher Gymnasium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wittelsbacher-Gymnasium_München \"Wittelsbacher-Gymnasium München\"). Munich has several notable international schools, including [Lycée Jean Renoir](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lycée_Jean_Renoir_(Munich) \"Lycée Jean Renoir (Munich)\"), the [Japanische Internationale Schule München](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Japanische_Internationale_Schule_München \"Japanische Internationale Schule München\"), the [Bavarian International School](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bavarian_International_School \"Bavarian International School\"), the [Munich International School](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Munich_International_School \"Munich International School\"), and the [European School, Munich](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/European_School,_Munich \"European School, Munich\").[*[citation needed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed \"Wikipedia:Citation needed\")*]\n\n### Scientific research institutions\n\n[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Munich&action=edit&section=73 \"Edit section: Scientific research institutions\")]\n\n### Max Planck Society\n\n[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Munich&action=edit&section=74 \"Edit section: Max Planck Society\")]\n\nThe [Max Planck Society](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Max_Planck_Society \"Max Planck Society\"), a government funded non-profit research organization, has its administrative headquarters in Munich.\n\n### Fraunhofer Society\n\n[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Munich&action=edit&section=75 \"Edit section: Fraunhofer Society\")]\n\nThe [Fraunhofer Society](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fraunhofer_Society \"Fraunhofer Society\"), the German government funded research organization for applied research, has its headquarters in Munich.\n\n### Other research institutes\n\n[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Munich&action=edit&section=76 \"Edit section: Other research institutes\")]\n\n* [Botanische Staatssammlung München](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Botanische_Staatssammlung_München \"Botanische Staatssammlung München\"), a notable [herbarium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Herbarium \"Herbarium\")\n* [Ifo Institute for Economic Research](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ifo_Institute_for_Economic_Research \"Ifo Institute for Economic Research\"), theoretical and applied research in economics and finance\n* [Doerner Institute](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Doerner_Institute \"Doerner Institute\")\n* [European Southern Observatory](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/European_Southern_Observatory \"European Southern Observatory\")\n* [Helmholtz Zentrum München](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Helmholtz_Zentrum_München \"Helmholtz Zentrum München\")\n* [Zoologische Staatssammlung München](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zoologische_Staatssammlung_München \"Zoologische Staatssammlung München\")\n* [German Aerospace Center](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/German_Aerospace_Center \"German Aerospace Center\") (DLR), [Oberpfaffenhofen bei München](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oberpfaffenhofen \"Oberpfaffenhofen\")\n\n## Transport\n\n[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Munich&action=edit&section=77 \"Edit section: Transport\")]\n\n[![](//thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/ae/Der_ZOB_in_M%C3%BCnchen_2017.jpg/250px-Der_ZOB_in_M%C3%BCnchen_2017.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Der_ZOB_in_München_2017.jpg)\n\nZOB Muenchen (long-distance bus terminal)\n\nMunich has an extensive public transport system consisting of an underground metro, trams, buses and high-speed rail. In 2015, the transport [modal share](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Modal_share \"Modal share\") in Munich was 38 percent public transport, 25 percent car, 23 percent walking, and 15 percent bicycle.[[212]](#cite_note-www.mvg.de-214) Its public transport system delivered 566 million passenger trips that year.[[213]](#cite_note-215)\n\nMunich is the hub of a developed regional transportation system, including the second-largest airport in Germany and the [Berlin–Munich high-speed railway](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Berlin–Munich_high-speed_railway \"Berlin–Munich high-speed railway\"), which connects Munich to the German capital city with a journey time of about 4 hours. [Flixmobility](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Flixmobility \"Flixmobility\") which offers intercity coach service is headquartered in Munich.\n\nThe trade fair [Transport Logistic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Transport_Logistic \"Transport Logistic\") is held every two years at the *Neue Messe München* (Messe München International).\n\n### Public transport\n\n[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Munich&action=edit&section=78 \"Edit section: Public transport\")]\n\n[![](//thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/a2/MVG_2168_Munchen_Ost_18-08-2007.jpg/250px-MVG_2168_Munchen_Ost_18-08-2007.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:MVG_2168_Munchen_Ost_18-08-2007.jpg)\n\nA class R2 *Straßenbahn* (*Tram*) on route 19 at Ostbahnhof\n\n [![](//thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/29/S-bahn-muenchen.jpg/250px-S-bahn-muenchen.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:S-bahn-muenchen.jpg)\n\n[Munich's S-Bahn](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Munich_S-Bahn \"Munich S-Bahn\") at the [Marienplatz station](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Munich_Marienplatz_station \"Munich Marienplatz station\")\n\nFor its urban population of 2.6 million people, Munich and its closest suburbs have a comprehensive network of public transport incorporating the [Munich U-Bahn](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Munich_U-Bahn \"Munich U-Bahn\"), the [Munich S-Bahn](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Munich_S-Bahn \"Munich S-Bahn\"), trams and buses. The system is supervised by the [Munich Transport and Tariff Association](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Munich_Transport_and_Tariff_Association \"Munich Transport and Tariff Association\") (*Münchner Verkehrs- und Tarifverbund*). The [Munich tramway](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Munich_tramway \"Munich tramway\") is the oldest existing public transportation system in the city, which has been in operation since 1876. Munich also has an extensive network of bus lines. The average amount of time people spend commuting to and from work with public transit in Munich on a weekday is 56 min.[*[citation needed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed \"Wikipedia:Citation needed\")*]\n\nThe extensive network of subway and tram lines assists and complement pedestrian movement in the city centre. The 700m-long Kaufinger Straße, which starts near the Main train station, forms a pedestrian east–west spine that traverses almost the entire centre. Major spines and many smaller streets cover an extensive area of the centre that can be enjoyed on foot and bike. These attributes result from applying the principle of [filtered permeability](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Permeability_(spatial_and_transport_planning) \"Permeability (spatial and transport planning)\"). Pedestrian and bike paths, which permeate the entire Munich city centre, go through public squares and open spaces for enjoyment. Munich city centre was subject to [urban planning](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Urban_planning \"Urban planning\") and has a comprehensive model for laying out neighborhoods and districts according to [grid plan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Grid_plan \"Grid plan\").[[214]](#cite_note-216)\n\n### Cycling\n\n[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Munich&action=edit&section=79 \"Edit section: Cycling\")]\n\nMain article: [Cycling in Munich](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cycling_in_Munich \"Cycling in Munich\")\n\nCycling has a strong presence in the city and is recognized as a good alternative. The growing number of [bicycle lanes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Segregated_cycle_facilities \"Segregated cycle facilities\") are widely used throughout the year. Cycle paths can be found alongside the majority of sidewalks and streets, although the newer or renovated ones are much easier to tell apart from pavements than older ones. A modern [bike hire system](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Call_a_Bike \"Call a Bike\") is available within the area bounded by the *Mittlerer Ring*.\n\n### Cultural history trails and bicycle routes\n\n[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Munich&action=edit&section=80 \"Edit section: Cultural history trails and bicycle routes\")]\n\nSince 2001, historically interesting places in Munich can be explored via the [List of cultural history trails in Munich](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_cultural_history_trails_in_Munich \"List of cultural history trails in Munich\") (*KulturGeschichtsPfade*). Sign-posted cycle routes are the Outer *Äußere Radlring* (outer cycle route) and the *RadlRing München*.[[215]](#cite_note-217)\n\n### Munich Central Train Station\n\n[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Munich&action=edit&section=81 \"Edit section: Munich Central Train Station\")]\n\nMain article: [München Hauptbahnhof](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/München_Hauptbahnhof \"München Hauptbahnhof\")\n\nMunich Central Train Station (*München Hauptbahnhof*) is the central railway station located in the city centre and is the long-distance station in Munich.[*[citation needed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed \"Wikipedia:Citation needed\")*][[216]](#cite_note-218)\n\nMunich Central Train Station serves about 450,000 passengers a day, which puts it on par with other large stations in Germany. Munich Central Train Station alongside [München Ost railway station](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/München_Ost_railway_station \"München Ost railway station\") are two of the 21 stations in Germany classified by [Deutsche Bahn](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Deutsche_Bahn \"Deutsche Bahn\") as a [category 1 station](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/German_railway_station_categories \"German railway station categories\").\n\nThe central mainline station is a [terminal station](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Terminal_station \"Terminal station\") with 32 [platforms](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Railway_platform \"Railway platform\"). The subterranean [S-Bahn](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Munich_S-Bahn \"Munich S-Bahn\") with 2 platforms and [U-Bahn](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Munich_U-Bahn \"Munich U-Bahn\") stations with 6 platforms are through stations.[*[citation needed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed \"Wikipedia:Citation needed\")*]\n\n[Intercity-Express](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Intercity-Express \"Intercity-Express\") (ICE) and [EuroCity-Express](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/EuroCity-Express \"EuroCity-Express\") (ECE; Munich–[Bregenz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bregenz \"Bregenz\")–[Zurich](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zurich \"Zurich\")) trains – and starting in 2026 also [TGV](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/TGV \"TGV\") trains (Munich–Paris)[[217]](#cite_note-219) – stop at Munich Central Train Station. [InterCity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/InterCity \"InterCity\") (IC) and [EuroCity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/EuroCity \"EuroCity\") (EC) trains to destinations east of Munich also stop at the München Ost railway station. Munich is connected to [Nuremberg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nuremberg \"Nuremberg\") via [Ingolstadt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ingolstadt \"Ingolstadt\") by the [Nuremberg–Munich high-speed railway](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nuremberg–Munich_high-speed_railway \"Nuremberg–Munich high-speed railway\") and [Berlin–Munich high-speed railway](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Berlin–Munich_high-speed_railway \"Berlin–Munich high-speed railway\").[*[citation needed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed \"Wikipedia:Citation needed\")*]\n\nThe old air raid shelter next to platform 11 of Munich Central Train Station was an important distribution point for [guest workers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Guest_worker \"Guest worker\") (*Gastarbeiter*) between 1960 and 1973. At peak more than 1,000 guest workers arrived per day, in total 1.8 million guest workers passed through Munich Central Train Station.[[218]](#cite_note-220)\n\n### Autobahns\n\n[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Munich&action=edit&section=82 \"Edit section: Autobahns\")]\n\n[![](//thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/26/Karte_Fernstra%C3%9Fen_M%C3%BCnchen.png/250px-Karte_Fernstra%C3%9Fen_M%C3%BCnchen.png?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Karte_Fernstraßen_München.png)\n\nMunich [motorway](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Autobahn \"Autobahn\") network\n\nMunich is an integral part of the [Autobahn](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Autobahn \"Autobahn\") network of southern Germany. Motorways from [Stuttgart](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stuttgart \"Stuttgart\") (W), [Nuremberg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nuremberg \"Nuremberg\"), [Frankfurt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Frankfurt_am_Main \"Frankfurt am Main\") and Berlin (N), [Deggendorf](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Deggendorf \"Deggendorf\") and [Passau](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Passau \"Passau\") (E), [Salzburg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Salzburg \"Salzburg\") and [Innsbruck](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Innsbruck \"Innsbruck\") (SE), [Garmisch Partenkirchen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Garmisch-Partenkirchen \"Garmisch-Partenkirchen\") (S) and [Lindau](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lindau \"Lindau\") (SW) terminate at Munich, allowing direct access to the different parts of Germany, Austria and Italy.\n\nTraffic is often very heavy in and around Munich. [Traffic congestion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Traffic_congestion \"Traffic congestion\") are commonplace at the beginning and end of major Bavarian holidays. There are few \"green waves\" or [roundabouts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roundabout \"Roundabout\"), and an abundance of construction sites.[*[citation needed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed \"Wikipedia:Citation needed\")*]\n\nMunich has introduced an [environmental zone](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Traffic_and_Environmental_Zone \"Traffic and Environmental Zone\") and was among the first German cities to require a [green sticker](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Green_sticker \"Green sticker\") for vehicles, these are a requirement when entering the city or driving in the wider surrounding area.[[219]](#cite_note-221)\n\n### Air\n\n[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Munich&action=edit&section=83 \"Edit section: Air\")]\n\n#### Munich International Airport\n\n[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Munich&action=edit&section=84 \"Edit section: Munich International Airport\")]\n\n[![](//thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/fd/Flughafen_turm.png/250px-Flughafen_turm.png?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Flughafen_turm.png)\n\n[Munich International Airport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Munich_Airport \"Munich Airport\") (MUC)\n\n[Franz Josef Strauss International Airport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Munich_Airport \"Munich Airport\") ([IATA](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_Air_Transport_Association \"International Air Transport Association\"): MUC, [ICAO](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_Civil_Aviation_Organization \"International Civil Aviation Organization\"): EDDM) is the second-largest airport in Germany and seventh-largest in Europe after [London Heathrow](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/London_Heathrow_Airport \"London Heathrow Airport\"), [Paris Charles de Gaulle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Charles_de_Gaulle_Airport \"Charles de Gaulle Airport\"), [Frankfurt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Frankfurt_International_Airport \"Frankfurt International Airport\"), [Amsterdam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amsterdam_Airport_Schiphol \"Amsterdam Airport Schiphol\"), [Madrid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Barajas_Airport \"Barajas Airport\") and [Istanbul Atatürk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Atatürk_International_Airport \"Atatürk International Airport\"). It is used by about 46 million passengers a year, and lies some 30 km (19 mi) north east of the city centre. It replaced the smaller [Munich-Riem Airport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Munich-Riem_Airport \"Munich-Riem Airport\") in 1992. The airport can be reached by suburban train lines from the city. From the [main railway station](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/München_Hauptbahnhof \"München Hauptbahnhof\") the journey takes 40–45 minutes. A [magnetic levitation train](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maglev_(transport) \"Maglev (transport)\") (called [Transrapid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Transrapid \"Transrapid\")), which was to have run at speeds of up to 400 km/h (249 mph) from the central station to the airport in a travel time of 10 minutes, had been approved,[[220]](#cite_note-222) but was cancelled in March 2008 because of cost escalation and after heavy protests.[[221]](#cite_note-223) [Lufthansa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lufthansa \"Lufthansa\") opened its second hub at the airport when Terminal 2 was opened in 2003.\n\n#### Other airports\n\n[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Munich&action=edit&section=85 \"Edit section: Other airports\")]\n\nIn 2008, the Bavarian state government granted a licence to expand Oberpfaffenhofen Air Station located west of Munich, for commercial use. These plans were opposed by many residents in the Oberpfaffenhofen area as well as other branches of local government, including the city of Munich, which took the case to court.[[222]](#cite_note-224) However, in October 2009, the permit allowing up to 9725 business flights per year to depart from or land at Oberpfaffenhofen was confirmed by a regional judge.[[223]](#cite_note-225)\n\nDespite being 110 km (68 mi) from Munich, [Memmingen Airport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Memmingen_Airport \"Memmingen Airport\") has been advertised as Airport Munich West. After 2005, passenger traffic of nearby [Augsburg Airport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Augsburg_Airport \"Augsburg Airport\") was relocated to Munich Airport, leaving the Augsburg region of Bavaria without an air passenger airport within close reach.\n\n## International relations\n\n[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Munich&action=edit&section=86 \"Edit section: International relations\")]\n\n### Twin towns and sister cities\n\n[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Munich&action=edit&section=87 \"Edit section: Twin towns and sister cities\")]\n\nSee also: [List of twin towns and sister cities in [Beersheba](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beersheba \"Beersheba\"), Israel (2021)\n\n## Around Munich\n\n[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Munich&action=edit&section=88 \"Edit section: Around Munich\")]\n\n### Nearby towns\n\n[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Munich&action=edit&section=89 \"Edit section: Nearby towns\")]\n\nThe Munich agglomeration sprawls across the plain of the [Alpine foothills](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Foothills \"Foothills\") comprising about 2.6 million inhabitants. Several smaller traditional Bavarian towns and cities like [Dachau](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dachau,_Bavaria \"Dachau, Bavaria\"), [Freising](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Freising \"Freising\"), [Erding](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Erding \"Erding\"), [Starnberg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Starnberg \"Starnberg\"), [Landshut](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Landshut \"Landshut\") and [Moosburg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Moosburg \"Moosburg\") are today part of the Greater Munich Region, formed by Munich and the surrounding districts, making up the [Munich Metropolitan Region](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Munich_Metropolitan_Region \"Munich Metropolitan Region\"), which has a population of about 6 million people.[[4]](#cite_note-mmr_official_site-5)\n\n### Recreation\n\n[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Munich&action=edit&section=90 \"Edit section: Recreation\")]\n\nSouth of Munich, there are numerous nearby freshwater lakes such as [Lake Starnberg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lake_Starnberg \"Lake Starnberg\"), [Ammersee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ammersee \"Ammersee\"), [Chiemsee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chiemsee \"Chiemsee\"), [Walchensee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Walchensee \"Walchensee\"), [Kochelsee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kochelsee \"Kochelsee\"), [Tegernsee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tegernsee_(lake) \"Tegernsee (lake)\"), [Schliersee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Schliersee_(lake) \"Schliersee (lake)\"), [Simssee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Simssee \"Simssee\"), [Staffelsee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Staffelsee \"Staffelsee\"), [Wörthsee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wörthsee \"Wörthsee\"), [Kirchsee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kirchsee_(Bavaria) \"Kirchsee (Bavaria)\") and the [Osterseen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Osterseen \"Osterseen\") (Easter Lakes), which are popular among Munich residents for recreation, swimming and watersports and can be quickly reached by car and a few also by Munich's [S-Bahn](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Munich_S-Bahn \"Munich
